APPENDIX I

GLOSSARY

AGGREGATE—Crushed rock or gravel screened to                    CURING—The process of keeping concrete damp
     size for use in road surfaces, concrete, or bitumi-             and at favorable temperatures to ensure complete
     nous mixes.                                                     hardening.

AIR-ENTRAINED CONCRETE—Concrete con-                            EXPANSION JOINT—Construction joint with
     taining millions of trapped air bubbles.                        expandable material at the contact points.

AUGER—A boring bit.                                             FINISHING NAILS—Twopenny (2d) to twenty-
                                                                     penny (20d) sizes with small barrel-shaped heads.
BATCH—The amount of concrete mixed at one time
     regardless of quantity.                                    FOOTING—An enlargement at the lower end of a
                                                                     wall to distribute the load to a wider area of
BATTER BOARDS—Two boards nailed at right                             supporting soil.
     angles to posts set up near the proposed corner of
     an excavation for a building and used for                  GIN POLE—An upright guy pole with hoisting
     transferring building lines.                                    tackle and foot-mounted snatch block used for
                                                                     vertical lifts.
BOX NAILS—Lightweight nails with large heads.
                                                                GIRDER—A supporting beam laid crosswise of the
BRAD—A slender nail with a small head.                               building; a long truss.

BRICK—Solid blocks of fine clay.                                GIRT—A horizontal brace used on outside walls
                                                                     covered with vertical siding.
BUTTERING—Putting mortar on a brick or block
     with a trowel before laying.                               GROUT—A mixture of sand, cement, and water that
                                                                     can be poured.
CARRIAGE BOLT—A partially threaded bolt with
     a head that is flat on the underside and rounded on        GUNITE—A patent name for spray concrete.
     top.
                                                                HONEYCOMBING—Sections of weak, porous
CASING NAILS—Twopenny (2d) to fortypenny                              concrete.
     (40d) nails with flaring heads.
                                                                JOIST—A member that makes up the body of the
CEMENT—Fuzed and pulverized limestone and                             floor and ceiling frames.
     clay.
                                                                LAG SCREW—A screw with a wrench head and
COMMON BOND—Five stretcher courses with the                           wood screw threads.
      sixth as an all header course.
                                                                LEADS—Points at which block and brick are laid up
COMMON NAILS—Twopenny (2d) to sixtypenny                              a few courses and used as guides.
      (60d) strong nails.
                                                                LINE—Strands of natural or synthetic fiber twisted
CONCRETE BUGGY—Two-wheeled buggy for                                  together, sometimes referred to as “rope.”
      transporting concrete, nicknamed “Georgia
      Buggy.”                                                   MONOLITHIC POUR—Concrete cast in a single
                                                                      pour.
CONCRETE—Artificial stone made of cement,
      water, sand, and aggregate.                               MORTAR—Sand, water, and cementing material in
                                                                      proper proportions.
CONSTRUCTION JOINT—A joint that runs
      through concrete. Made by pouring sections of a           MOUSING—Turns of cordage around the opening of
      structure at different times.                                   a block hook.

COURSE—A single layer of bricks, stone, or other                PERLITE—Lightweight concrete aggregate.
      masonry.

PUMP CREATE—A method of placing small                     SLUMP TEST—A means of sample testing concrete
     aggregate concrete by means of a pump.                    for consistency; a measure of the plasticity of a
                                                               concrete mix.
PURLINS—Horizontal members of a roof sup-
     porting the common rafters. The members span         SLURRY—Thin watery mixture of water and
     between trusses to support sheeting.                      cement.

PUTLOG—Horizontal boards set perpendicular to             STRIPPING—The removal of mold forms from
     scaffold lengths that directly support the platform       hardened concrete.
     planks.
                                                          STUDS—The vertical members of walls, wooden
RAFTERS—Main body members of roof frame-                       forms, and frames.
     work.
                                                          TERRAZZO—A concrete surface of Portland
REEVING—Threading or placement of a working                    cement, fines, and marble chips.
     line.
                                                          TIES—Metal strips used to tie the outer wall of brick
RIDGEBOARD—The horizontal timber at the upper                  or masonry to the inner wall. Also used to tie
     end of the common rafters to which the rafters are        concrete forms together.
     nailed.
                                                          TRUSS—A combination of members, such as beams,
SHEAVE—A grooved wheel used to support cable or                bars, and ties, usually arranged in triangular units
     change its direction of travel (pronounced                to form a rigid framework for supporting loads
     “shiv”).                                                  over a span, usually a roof or bridge.

SHRINKAGE—Concrete contraction due to curing              WIRE ROPE—A rope formed of wires wrapped
     and excess water in mix.                                  around a central core; a steel cable.
